So... I think I might lose that opportunity. Which is fine! I mean, I understand why he's worried. 

And, um... at home... my husband... he's been so patient, he really is wonderful. But he did mention that I seem like I'm barely there lately. Like I'm just not present with him anymore. 

I'm really trying to fix it, though! I promise I'm working on being more focused. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee or a bad wife... I just want to be honest with you so you can help me. Is... is that the kind of information you needed?

Um... well, I know it sounds really silly when I say it out loud. I really don't want you to think I'm, you know, losing my mind or being a difficult person. But... yes, to be completely honest, I guess I do believe it pretty strongly. 

When I'm at the computer, I just keep feeling like those couple of servers and that one regular customer are watching exactly how I ring things in. And then I look at the order tickets, and it's like... the way they loop certain letters or leave these specific little marks... it just feels so obvious to me that they're doing it on purpose. Like they're leaving those little signs to prove I'm messing up, just building this whole case to get me fired. 

I really try to just brush it off, because like I said, my husband keeps telling me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups and that everyone just has sloppy handwriting when it's busy. And I really want to believe him! I know he's probably right, and I'm trying so hard to see it his way so I don't cause any drama at work. I want to be a team player. But in the moment... it just feels so incredibly real to me. 

I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds so paranoid and terrible. Am I... is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and answering what you actually need to know.

Like they're leaving those little signs to prove I'm messing up, just building this whole case to get me fired. \n\nI really try to just brush it off, because like I said, my husband keeps telling me it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups and that everyone just has sloppy handwriting when it's busy. And I really want to believe him! I know he's probably right, and I'm trying so hard to see it his way so I don't cause any drama at work. I want to be a team player. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I mean, I really do try to doubt it. I try so, so hard to just listen to my husband, because he's so smart and level-headed. And I know it makes me sound awful to say I don't believe him. 

But... oh gosh, I hate admitting this... even when he tells me it's just messy handwriting and normal restaurant mistakes, I... I still just feel so certain about it. It's like, no matter how much I tell myself to just be normal and let it go, I still have this overwhelming feeling that those servers and that one customer are watching me ring things in. And those little signs on the tickets... it just feels so real that they're doing it to get me fired. 

I really am trying to stop thinking that way, I promise! I don't want to be the kind of person who thinks badly of their coworkers. I know I should just trust my husband... does it make me a really difficult patient that I can't just shake the feeling? I hope I'm not frustrating you by saying the same thing again... I just want to be totally honest so I'm doing this right.

I don't want to be the kind of person who thinks badly of their coworkers. I know I should just trust my husband... does it make me a really difficult patient that I can't just shake the feeling? I hope I'm not frustrating you by saying the same thing again... I just want to be totally honest so I'm doing this right.\n\n[Last Question]\nEven though others have told you that the handwriting on the tickets is normal and that you’re not doing anything wrong, do you still feel completely certain that these marks are intentional and part of a plan to get you fired?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... I'm so, so sorry, Doctor. I feel terrible that you have to keep asking me the exact same thing. I must be doing a really bad job of explaining myself, or... or maybe I'm just being a incredibly frustrating patient today. I promise I'm not trying to be difficult!

{
  "candidates": ["F22", "F23", "F21", "F41.9"],
  "note": "F20 and F25 were ruled out due to absence of prominent delusions or hallucinations outside the described voice that could indicate schizophrenia or schizoaffective disorder. The patient maintains insight regarding the voice (attributing it to stress), which further reduces the likelihood of a psychotic disorder. The persistent, stress-related belief about coworkers and the regular customer intentionally trying to get her fired, despite reassurance, suggests a delusional theme but without full delusional conviction or broader psychotic features. This supports exclusion of F20 and F25. F22 (persistent delusional disorder) remains possible if the preoccupation with the coworkers/customer is judged to meet delusional criteria, while F23 (acute and transient psychotic disorders) remains due to possible early or subthreshold psychotic phenomena.",
